<<<FILE path="MORPHOLOGY.md" plugin="docs" lang="markdown" reason="extension:.md" lines="1195" bytes="37406" original_lines="1195" original_bytes="37406" minified="false" sha256="fcd182a3932225a2872d58a24104952d6c46a71007b7b1ebc3e2cef9dc6b4af1">>>
# Morphology — Algorithmic System

Morphology in lexFlex is **algorithmic, not tabular**. Word forms are generated by applying rules to stems, not looked up in hardcoded tables. Exceptions are explicitly listed; everything else follows paradigm rules.

**2026-07-10 Iterative Audit (docs updated in parallel with ERRORS.MD and code review):**
While the philosophy is correct, implementation has significant hardcoded remnants (detailed in lexFlex/ERRORS.MD — see the new "Full Cross-Language (PL + EN) Non-Algorithmic Elements + Linguistic Theory Gaps Audit" section for the exhaustive enumerated list with file:line quotes):
- Degree for irregular comparatives (lepszy, większy...) uses Rust match lists + parser heuristics instead of RON.
- "jabłko" gen.pl patch is a specific if in generator, not a rule.
- Article phonetics (a/an) live in generator code with simplistic checks + cross-lang name hacks.
- Gender inference and paradigm selection mix code ifs with data.
- Passive/verb forms have ending lists and irregular matches.
- Parsers still use crude ends_with + matches! for degree/person before (or instead of) full `reverse_to_stem` + `analyze_via_paradigms`.
- No systematic suppletive or lemma-linked exception tables.

**Required low-level engines (see full list in ERRORS.MD):**
- Bidirectional Stemmer/Analyzer (make analyze_via_paradigms + reverse primary path).
- PhonologyEngine (separate from morph; phonetic classes for articles + alternations).
- Suppletion/Exception engine + richer RON (Prefix, SuppletiveStem, PhoneticInitial conditions).

**RON extensions required for "strictly algorithmic"**:
- PhoneticInitial / vowel class feature (lexicon or computed) for article choice.
- Suppletive stems + Prefix ops (naj-) + better exception linking in paradigms.
- Conditions that allow degree to compose cleanly with case/gender without code bypass.
- Surface variant / mapping data separate from code (to kill the "duży"->"big" etc. contains/replaces).
- Virile / sub-gender composition rules.

See ERRORS.MD (new comprehensive PL+EN + theory gaps section + "Required Low-Level Algorithmic Engines") and UNIFIED... . This file updated concurrently. Next iterations must remove the listed hacks by extending data/rules + minimal inference algos (ending-based where possible). Always re-grep after changes.

## Core Principle

> **Rules generate forms. Exceptions override rules. Paradigms group rules.**

A paradigm is a set of ordered rules that, given a stem + feature bundle, produce the correct surface form. This mirrors how linguists describe morphology — and makes it possible to add new words without writing out every form.

---

## Architecture

```
FeatureBundle + Lemma
         │
         ▼
┌─────────────────────────┐
│ PARADIGM SELECTOR       │  ← lexicon entry specifies paradigm class
│ "ać" verb paradigm      │
└────────────┬────────────┘
             ▼
┌─────────────────────────┐
│ RULE ENGINE             │  ← ordered rules: stem modification + suffix
│ Rule 1: stem change     │
│ Rule 2: add suffix      │
│ Rule 3: orthographic fix│
└────────────┬────────────┘
             ▼
┌─────────────────────────┐
│ EXCEPTION CHECK         │  ← explicit overrides for irregular forms
│ "dać" + 1sg.past → "dałem"│
└────────────┬────────────┘
             ▼
         Surface Form
```

---

## Paradigm Definition

```rust
struct Paradigm {
    name: String,                          // "verb_ać", "noun_ka"
    applies_to: PartOfSpeech,
    rules: Vec<MorphRule>,                 // ordered rules
    exceptions: Vec<MorphException>,       // explicit overrides
}

struct MorphRule {
    conditions: Vec<Condition>,            // when does this rule apply?
    operations: Vec<Operation>,            // what does it do to the stem?
}

// A condition checks features of the target form
enum Condition {
    TenseIs(Tense),
    AspectIs(Aspect),
    PersonIs(Person),
    NumberIs(Number),
    GenderIs(Gender),
    CaseIs(Case),
    AnimacyIs(Animacy),
    StemEndsWith(String),                  // phonological condition
    StemLength(Min, Max),                  // e.g., for vowel alternations
}

// An operation modifies the stem or adds affixes
enum Operation {
    AddSuffix(String),                     // stem + "łem"
    AddPrefix(String),                     // "nie" + stem (not used in inflection)
    ReplaceSuffix { from: String, to: String },  // "ać" → "am"
    VowelAlternate { from: Vowel, to: Vowel },   // o → ó (Polish vowel shift)
    ConsonantAlternate { from: String, to: String }, // k → c (palatalization)
    Truncate(usize),                       // remove N chars from stem end
    StressShift(usize),                    // not used in PL (fixed penultimate)
}
```

---

## Polish Noun Declension

### Paradigm Classes

Polish nouns fall into declension classes based on gender and stem ending:

| Class | Gender | Stem Ending | Example |
|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| `o` | Neuter | -o, -e, -ę | jabłk**o**, mies**e** |
| `a_fem` | Feminine | -a, -i | książk**a**, chwil**a** |
| `a_masc` | Masculine | -a (male persons) | sędzi**a** |
| `consonant_hard` | Masc (inanimate) | hard consonant | bank, dom, stó**ł** |
| `consonant_soft` | Masc (animate) | soft consonant | ko**ń**, li**ś** |
| `consonant_personal` | Masc (personal) | consonant | chłop**ak**, brat |
| `zero_fem` | Feminine | zero ending | noc, miło**ś**ć |

### Noun Paradigm: "o" (Neuter, e.g., "jabłko")

```rust
// data/morphology/pl/noun_paradigms.ron

Paradigm(
    name: "noun_o",
    applies_to: Noun,
    rules: [
        // ─── SINGULAR ──────────────────────────────
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [CaseIs(Nominative), NumberIs(Singular)],
            operations: [],                            // jabłko → jabłko
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [CaseIs(Genitive), NumberIs(Singular)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "o", to: "a" },  // jabłko → jabłka
            ],
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [CaseIs(Dative), NumberIs(Singular)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "o", to: "u" },  // jabłko → jabłku
            ],
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [CaseIs(Accusative), NumberIs(Singular)],
            operations: [],                            // neuter: ACC = NOM
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [CaseIs(Instrumental), NumberIs(Singular)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "o", to: "em" }, // jabłko → jabłkiem
            ],
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [CaseIs(Locative), NumberIs(Singular)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "o", to: "u" },  // jabłko → jabłku
            ],
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [CaseIs(Vocative), NumberIs(Singular)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "o", to: "o" },  // jabłko → jabłko
            ],
        ),

        // ─── PLURAL ────────────────────────────────
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [CaseIs(Nominative), NumberIs(Plural)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "o", to: "a" },  // jabłko → jabłka
            ],
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [CaseIs(Genitive), NumberIs(Plural)],
            operations: [
                Truncate(1),                           // remove "o"
                AddSuffix("ek"),                       // jabłko → jabłek (with insertion)
                // Note: insertion of "e" is a sub-rule for clusters
            ],
        ),
        // ... Dative, Accusative, Instrumental, Locative for plural
    ],
    exceptions: [],    // "jabłko" has no irregular forms
)
```

### Noun Paradigm: "a_fem" (Feminine, e.g., "książka")

```rust
Paradigm(
    name: "noun_ka",
    applies_to: Noun,
    rules: [
        // Singular
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [CaseIs(Nominative), NumberIs(Singular)],
            operations: [],                            // książka → książka
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [CaseIs(Genitive), NumberIs(Singular)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "ka", to: "ki" },// książka → książki
            ],
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [CaseIs(Dative), NumberIs(Singular)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "ka", to: "ce" },// książka → książce
                // Note: k → c palatalization before "e"
            ],
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [CaseIs(Accusative), NumberIs(Singular)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "a", to: "ę" }, // książka → książkę
            ],
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [CaseIs(Instrumental), NumberIs(Singular)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "a", to: "ą" }, // książka → książką
            ],
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [CaseIs(Locative), NumberIs(Singular)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "ka", to: "ce" },// książka → (o) książce
            ],
        ),

        // Plural
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [CaseIs(Nominative), NumberIs(Plural)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "ka", to: "ki" },// książka → książki
            ],
        ),
        // ... etc
    ],
    exceptions: [],
)
```

### Noun with Irregular Forms: "człowiek" (person)

```rust
Paradigm(
    name: "noun_consonant_personal",
    applies_to: Noun,
    rules: [ /* standard hard-consonant masculine rules */ ],
    exceptions: [
        // "człowiek" has suppletive plural: "ludzie"
        MorphException(
            conditions: [NumberIs(Plural)],
            override_stem: "ludzi",           // replaces entire stem for all plural cases
            // Then regular plural case endings apply to "ludzi-" stem
        ),
    ],
)
```

---

## Polish Verb Conjugation

### Paradigm Classes

| Class | Ending | Example | Count |
|-------|--------|---------|-------|
| `ać` | -ać | robi**ć** (→ robi-), czyta**ć** | Most common |
| `eć` | -eć | widzi**eć**, siedzi**eć** | |
| `iść` | -iść | iś**ć**, nieś**ć** | Small class |
| `ować` | -ować | prac**ować**, kup**ować** | Productive (borrowings) |
| `nąć` | -nąć | krzyk**nąć**, znik**nąć** | |
| `irregular` | — | być, mieć, dać, iść, jeść | Handful |

### Verb Paradigm: "ać" (e.g., "czytać" — to read)

```rust
// data/morphology/pl/verb_paradigms.ron

Paradigm(
    name: "verb_ać",
    applies_to: Verb,
    rules: [
        // ─── PRESENT ───────────────────────────────
        // Stem: czyt- (remove -ać)
        
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Present), PersonIs(First), NumberIs(Singular)],
            operations: [
                Truncate(2),                   // "czytać" → "czyt"
                AddSuffix("am"),               // → "czytam"
            ],
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Present), PersonIs(Second), NumberIs(Singular)],
            operations: [
                Truncate(2),
                AddSuffix("asz"),              // → "czytasz"
            ],
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Present), PersonIs(Third), NumberIs(Singular)],
            operations: [
                Truncate(2),
                AddSuffix("a"),                // → "czyta"
            ],
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Present), PersonIs(First), NumberIs(Plural)],
            operations: [
                Truncate(2),
                AddSuffix("amy"),              // → "czytamy"
            ],
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Present), PersonIs(Second), NumberIs(Plural)],
            operations: [
                Truncate(2),
                AddSuffix("acie"),             // → "czytacie"
            ],
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Present), PersonIs(Third), NumberIs(Plural)],
            operations: [
                Truncate(2),
                AddSuffix("ają"),              // → "czytają"
            ],
        ),

        // ─── PAST ──────────────────────────────────
        // Past tense in PL encodes gender in singular
        
        // 1sg masculine: "czytałem"
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Past), PersonIs(First), NumberIs(Singular), GenderIs(Masculine)],
            operations: [
                Truncate(2),                   // "czytać" → "czyt"
                AddSuffix("ałem"),             // → "czytałem"
            ],
        ),
        // 1sg feminine: "czytałam"
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Past), PersonIs(First), NumberIs(Singular), GenderIs(Feminine)],
            operations: [
                Truncate(2),
                AddSuffix("ałam"),             // → "czytałam"
            ],
        ),
        // 2sg masculine: "czytałeś"
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Past), PersonIs(Second), NumberIs(Singular), GenderIs(Masculine)],
            operations: [
                Truncate(2),
                AddSuffix("ałeś"),
            ],
        ),
        // 2sg feminine: "czytałaś"
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Past), PersonIs(Second), NumberIs(Singular), GenderIs(Feminine)],
            operations: [
                Truncate(2),
                AddSuffix("ałaś"),
            ],
        ),
        // 3sg masculine: "czytał"
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Past), PersonIs(Third), NumberIs(Singular), GenderIs(Masculine)],
            operations: [
                Truncate(2),
                AddSuffix("ał"),               // → "czytał"
            ],
        ),
        // 3sg feminine: "czytała"
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Past), PersonIs(Third), NumberIs(Singular), GenderIs(Feminine)],
            operations: [
                Truncate(2),
                AddSuffix("ała"),              // → "czytała"
            ],
        ),
        // 3sg neuter: "czytało"
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Past), PersonIs(Third), NumberIs(Singular), GenderIs(Neuter)],
            operations: [
                Truncate(2),
                AddSuffix("ało"),              // → "czytało"
            ],
        ),
        // 1pl: "czytaliśmy" (virile) / "czytałyśmy" (non-virile)
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Past), PersonIs(First), NumberIs(Plural), GenderIs(MasculinePersonal)],
            operations: [
                Truncate(2),
                AddSuffix("aliśmy"),
            ],
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Past), PersonIs(First), NumberIs(Plural)],
            // non-virile (all other genders in plural)
            operations: [
                Truncate(2),
                AddSuffix("ałyśmy"),
            ],
        ),
        // ... 2pl, 3pl similar
        
        // ─── FUTURE (analytic for imperfective) ────
        // Future imperfective = "będę" + infinitive or past form
        // This is handled at the syntax level, not morphology
        // "będę czytał" or "będę czytać"
        
        // ─── IMPERATIVE ────────────────────────────
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [MoodIs(Imperative), PersonIs(Second), NumberIs(Singular)],
            operations: [
                Truncate(2),
                AddSuffix("aj"),               // → "czytaj!"
            ],
        ),
    ],
    exceptions: [],
)
```

### Irregular Verb: "dać" (to give, perfective)

```rust
Paradigm(
    name: "verb_dać_irreg",
    applies_to: Verb,
    rules: [
        // Present/future (perfective present = future meaning)
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Present), PersonIs(First), NumberIs(Singular)],
            operations: [
                Truncate(2),                   // "dać" → "d"
                AddSuffix("am"),               // → "dam"
            ],
        ),
        // ... other present forms follow regular-ish pattern
    ],
    exceptions: [
        // Past tense is irregular
        MorphException(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Past), PersonIs(Third), NumberIs(Singular), GenderIs(Masculine)],
            override: "dał",                   // not *"dał" from rules — actually this is regular
            // The irregularity is in the vowel: "dał" vs expected form
        ),
        MorphException(
            conditions: [MoodIs(Imperative), PersonIs(Second), NumberIs(Singular)],
            override: "daj",                   // not *"daj" from "da-" stem
        ),
    ],
)
```

---

## Adjective Agreement

Adjectives in Polish agree with their noun in **gender, number, and case**. The morphology engine applies the same paradigm-based approach.

### Adjective Paradigm: standard "-y" (e.g., "duży" — big)

```rust
// data/morphology/pl/adj_paradigms.ron

Paradigm(
    name: "adj_y",
    applies_to: Adjective,
    rules: [
        // ─── SINGULAR ──────────────────────────────
        // Masculine
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [GenderIs(Masculine), NumberIs(Singular), CaseIs(Nominative)],
            operations: [],                            // duży → duży
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [GenderIs(Masculine), NumberIs(Singular), CaseIs(Genitive)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "y", to: "ego" },// duży → dużego
            ],
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [GenderIs(Masculine), NumberIs(Singular), CaseIs(Dative)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "y", to: "emu" },// duży → dużemu
            ],
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [GenderIs(Masculine), AnimacyIs(Animate), NumberIs(Singular), CaseIs(Accusative)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "y", to: "ego" },// duży kot → dużego kota
            ],
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [GenderIs(Masculine), AnimacyIs(Inanimate), NumberIs(Singular), CaseIs(Accusative)],
            operations: [],                            // duży dom → duży dom (ACC=NOM for inanimate)
        ),
        
        // Feminine
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [GenderIs(Feminine), NumberIs(Singular), CaseIs(Nominative)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "y", to: "a" },  // duży → duża
            ],
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [GenderIs(Feminine), NumberIs(Singular), CaseIs(Accusative)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "y", to: "ą" },  // duży → dużą
            ],
        ),
        
        // Neuter
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [GenderIs(Neuter), NumberIs(Singular), CaseIs(Nominative)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "y", to: "e" },  // duży → duże
            ],
        ),
        
        // ─── PLURAL ────────────────────────────────
        // Virile (masculine personal plural)
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [GenderIs(MasculinePersonal), NumberIs(Plural), CaseIs(Nominative)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "y", to: "y" },  // duży → duzi (with consonant shift: ż → z)
                ConsonantAlternate { from: "ż", to: "z" },
            ],
        ),
        // Non-virile plural
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [NumberIs(Plural), CaseIs(Nominative)],
            // non-virile: uses "e" ending
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "y", to: "e" },  // duży → duże
            ],
        ),
    ],
    exceptions: [],
)
```

### Comparative / Superlative (Status: Planned / Partially documented, not in core code as of 2026-07-10)

See [UNIFIED_ALGORITHMIC_GENERATION_PIPELINE.md](./UNIFIED_ALGORITHMIC_GENERATION_PIPELINE.md) and [LANGUAGE_DESCRIPTOR.md](./LANGUAGE_DESCRIPTOR.md) for integration with the common pipeline.

Current adj_paradigms only cover case/gender/number (adj_y). Degree support (Positive/Comparative/Superlative) + exceptions for suppletives (dobry→lepszy) are in design but not wired in FeatureBundle/Condition/morph apply or generators.

```rust
// Planned: add DegreeIs to conditions
MorphRule(
    conditions: [DegreeIs(Comparative)],
    operations: [ReplaceSuffix(from: "y", to: "szy"), ConsonantAlternate(...) ],  // lekki → lżejszy
)
```

Exceptions for true irregulars will be used (see MorphException in src/data/morphology.rs, currently unused).

"bardzo dobry" is periphrastic (handled in NP realizer, not pure morph).

---

## English Morphology

English morphology is simpler (analytic language) but still algorithmic:

### Verb: Regular "-ed"

```rust
// data/morphology/en/verb_paradigms.ron

Paradigm(
    name: "verb_regular",
    applies_to: Verb,
    rules: [
        // Present
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Present), PersonIs(Third), NumberIs(Singular)],
            operations: [AddSuffix("s")],      // walk → walks
        ),
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Present)],
            operations: [],                    // walk → walk (all other forms)
        ),
        
        // Past
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Past)],
            operations: [AddSuffix("ed")],     // walk → walked
        ),
        
        // Present participle
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [AspectIs(Progressive)],
            operations: [AddSuffix("ing")],    // walk → walking
        ),
    ],
    exceptions: [
        // Spelling rules as exceptions
        MorphException(
            conditions: [TenseIs(Past), StemEndsWith("e")],
            override_suffix: "d",              // love → loved (not *"loveed")
        ),
        MorphException(
            conditions: [AspectIs(Progressive), StemEndsWith("e")],
            operations: [
                Truncate(1),                   // remove "e"
                AddSuffix("ing"),              // love → loving
            ],
        ),
        // CVC doubling: stop → stopped, stopping
        MorphException(
            conditions: [StemPattern(CVC_Short_Vowel)],
            // double final consonant before -ed, -ing
            operations: [DoubleFinalConsonant, AddSuffix("ed")],
        ),
    ],
)
```

### Noun: Plural

```rust
Paradigm(
    name: "noun_regular",
    applies_to: Noun,
    rules: [
        MorphRule(
            conditions: [NumberIs(Plural)],
            operations: [AddSuffix("s")],      // cat → cats
        ),
    ],
    exceptions: [
        MorphException(
            conditions: [NumberIs(Plural), StemEndsWith("s"), StemEndsWith("sh"), StemEndsWith("ch"), StemEndsWith("x")],
            override_suffix: "es",             // bus → buses, box → boxes
        ),
        MorphException(
            conditions: [NumberIs(Plural), StemEndsWith("y"), StemPrecedesY(Consonant)],
            operations: [
                ReplaceSuffix { from: "y", to: "ies" },  // city → cities
            ],
        ),
    ],
)
```

---

## Morphology in the Generation Pipeline

```
Interlingua: Frame::TRANSFER {
    agent: Entity{name:"Tomek", gender:M, number:Sg}
    theme: Entity{concept:APPLE, gender:N, number:Sg}
}
tense: PAST, aspect: PERFECTIVE

Generation steps:
│
├─→ 1. Select lemma for each entity from lexicon
│      agent → "Tomek" (proper noun, no declension)
│      theme → "jabłko" (noun, paradigm: noun_o)
│      predicate → "dać" (verb, paradigm: verb_dać_irreg)
│
├─→ 2. Determine case for each role (from GRAMMAR_CASES)
│      agent (NOM) → "Tomek" (masc personal, NOM.SG)
│      theme (ACC) → "jabłko" (neuter, ACC.SG = NOM form)
│
├─→ 3. Apply morphological rules
│      "Tomek" + NOM.SG → "Tomek"
│      "jabłko" + ACC.SG → "jabłko" (rule: neuter ACC = NOM)
│      "dać" + PAST.3SG.M → "dał"
│
├─→ 4. Agreement: adjectives match their noun
│      (no adjectives in this sentence)
│
└─→ Output: "Tomek dał jabłko"
```

---

## Phonological Sub-Rules

Some morphological operations trigger phonological changes:

### Polish Vowel Alternations

```rust
// These apply AFTER morphological operations as cleanup passes

PhonologicalRule(
    name: "o_to_ó",
    conditions: [StressOnSyllable(-2)],        // ó appears in stressed syllables
    operation: VowelAlternate { from: "o", to: "ó" },
    // Example: róg (NOM) → rogu (GEN) — the ó alternates with o
)

PhonologicalRule(
    name: "e_to_zero",
    conditions: [BeforeSuffix],                 // mobile e disappears
    operation: Truncate(1),
    // Example: pies (NOM) → psa (GEN) — the e disappears
)
```

### Polish Consonant Alternations

```rust
// Palatalization before front vowels (e, i)
PhonologicalRule(
    name: "palatalization",
    conditions: [BeforeVowel("e"), BeforeVowel("i")],
    operations: [
        ConsonantAlternate { from: "k", to: "c" },   // ręka → ręce
        ConsonantAlternate { from: "g", to: "dz" },  // noga → nodze
        ConsonantAlternate { from: "t", to: "ć" },   // kot → kocie (loc)
        ConsonantAlternate { from: "d", to: "dz" },  // gwiazda → gwieździe
        ConsonantAlternate { from: "n", to: "ń" },   // żona → żonie
    ],
)
```

These are stored in `data/morphology/pl/phonological_rules.ron` and applied as a post-processing step after all paradigm rules.

---

## Exception Philosophy

Exceptions exist but should be **minimized**. Before adding an exception:

1. Can a more specific rule cover it? (prefer rule over exception)
2. Is it truly irregular, or just a different paradigm class?
3. Can a phonological sub-rule handle it?

```
Truly irregular (needs exception):
  • "dać" → "daj" (imperative) — no rule predicts this
  • "iść" → "szedł" (past) — suppletive stem
  • "być" → all forms — fully irregular

Different paradigm class (NOT an exception):
  • "widzieć" follows "eć" paradigm, not "ać" — just a different class
  • "mięso" follows "o" paradigm — straightforward

Phonological rule (NOT an exception):
  • "pies" → "psa" — mobile e rule covers this
  • "ręka" → "ręce" — palatalization rule covers this
```

---

## Allomorphy

Allomorphs are variant forms of the same morpheme that occur in different environments. They are predictable from context.

### Types of Allomorphy

**1. Phonologically Conditioned Allomorphy**

The form is determined by the phonological environment.

```rust
pub enum AllomorphType {
    /// Different forms based on surrounding sounds
    Phonological {
        environment: PhonologicalEnvironment,
        form: String,
    },
}

pub enum PhonologicalEnvironment {
    BeforeVowel,
    BeforeConsonant,
    WordFinal,
    AfterNasal,
    AfterSibilant,
}
```

**Example: English plural -s**

```
PhonologicalEnvironment → Allomorph:
  After voiceless consonant → /s/  (cats /kæts/)
  After voiced consonant    → /z/  (dogs /dɒɡz/)
  After sibilant            → /ɪz/ (buses /bʌsɪz/)
```

**Example: Polish instrumental plural**

```
After hard consonant → -ami  (kotami)
After soft consonant → -mi   (końmi)
After -a           → -ami   (kotami)
```

**2. Morphologically Conditioned Allomorphy**

The form is determined by the morphological context (specific words or paradigms).

```rust
pub struct MorphologicalAllomorph {
    /// Which lexemes trigger this allomorph
    triggers: Vec<String>,
    /// The allomorphic form
    form: String,
}
```

**Example: Polish "ten" (this)**

```
Masculine: ten (ten kot)
Feminine:  ta (ta książka)
Neuter:    to (to jabłko)
Plural:    ci (ci studenci) / te (te książki)
```

These are not predictable by phonology — they're stored as allomorphs in the paradigm.

**3. Lexically Conditioned Allomorphy**

Specific words have unique allomorphs that must be listed.

```rust
pub struct LexicalAllomorph {
    /// The lexeme that has this allomorph
    lexeme: String,
    /// The allomorphic form
    form: String,
    /// Context (case, number, etc.)
    context: FeatureBundle,
}
```

**Example: Polish "człowiek" (person)**

```
Singular: człowiek (NOM), człowieka (GEN), człowiekowi (DAT)
Plural:   ludzie (NOM) — suppletive!
          ludzi (GEN)
          ludziom (DAT)
```

The plural stem "ludzi-" is completely different from singular "człowiek-". This is suppletion (see below).

---

## Suppletion

Suppletion occurs when different forms of a word come from **completely different historical roots**. These forms cannot be predicted by rules and must be listed explicitly.

### Representing Suppletion

```rust
pub struct SuppletiveParadigm {
    /// Base form (citation form)
    pub base: String,
    
    /// Suppletive stems for different contexts
    pub stems: Vec<SuppletiveStem>,
}

pub struct SuppletiveStem {
    /// When to use this stem
    pub conditions: Vec<Condition>,
    /// The suppletive stem
    pub stem: String,
}
```

### Common Suppletive Words (Polish)

**1. "iść" (to go) — verb suppletion**

```
Present: idę, idziesz, idzie (stem: id-)
Past:    szedłem, szedłaś, szedł (stem: szedł-)
Future:  pójdę, pójdziesz, pójdzie (stem: pójd-)
```

```rust
SuppletiveParadigm {
    base: "iść",
    stems: vec![
        SuppletiveStem {
            conditions: vec![TenseIs(Present)],
            stem: "id",
        },
        SuppletiveStem {
            conditions: vec![TenseIs(Past)],
            stem: "szedł",
        },
        SuppletiveStem {
            conditions: vec![TenseIs(Future)],
            stem: "pójd",
        },
    ],
}
```

**2. "być" (to be) — highly suppletive**

```
Present: jestem, jesteś, jest (stem: jest-)
Past:    byłem, byłaś, był (stem: by-)
Future:  będę, będziesz, będzie (stem: będ-)
```

**3. "dobry" (good) — adjective suppletion**

```
Positive:  dobry (good)
Comparative: lepszy (better) — different root!
Superlative: najlepszy (best)
```

```rust
SuppletiveParadigm {
    base: "dobry",
    stems: vec![
        SuppletiveStem {
            conditions: vec![DegreeIs(Positive)],
            stem: "dobr",
        },
        SuppletiveStem {
            conditions: vec![DegreeIs(Comparative)],
            stem: "leps",
        },
        SuppletiveStem {
            conditions: vec![DegreeIs(Superlative)],
            stem: "najleps",
        },
    ],
}
```

**4. "dużo" (much) — adverb suppletion**

```
Positive:  dużo (much)
Comparative: więcej (more) — different root!
Superlative: najwięcej (most)
```

### Common Suppletive Words (English)

| Base | Comparative | Superlative |
|------|-------------|-------------|
| good | better | best |
| bad | worse | worst |
| much/many | more | most |
| little | less | least |
| far | farther/further | farthest/furthest |

| Base | Past | Past Participle |
|------|------|-----------------|
| go | went | gone |
| be | was/were | been |
| do | did | done |
| have | had | had |

---

## Zero Morphology

Some grammatical categories are expressed by **no overt marking** — the form is identical to the base form. This is called zero morphology or null inflection.

### Zero Marking in Polish

**1. Neuter nouns: ACC = NOM**

```
jabłko (NOM) = jabłko (ACC)
dziecko (NOM) = dziecko (ACC)
```

```rust
MorphRule(
    conditions: [
        GenderIs(Neuter),
        CaseIs(Accusative),
        NumberIs(Singular),
    ],
    operations: [],  // No change!
)
```

**2. Inanimate masculine nouns: ACC = NOM**

```
dom (NOM) = dom (ACC)
kot (NOM) = kot (ACC)
```

```rust
MorphRule(
    conditions: [
        GenderIs(Masculine),
        AnimacyIs(Inanimate),
        CaseIs(Accusative),
        NumberIs(Singular),
    ],
    operations: [],  // No change!
)
```

**3. 3rd person singular neuter past: -ło**

```
czytać → czytało (it read)
```

The ending -ło is the overt marker, but for some verbs the stem doesn't change:

```
dać → dało (it gave) — stem "da-" unchanged
```

### Zero Marking in English

**1. Plural: zero for some nouns**

```
sheep (SG) = sheep (PL)
deer (SG) = deer (PL)
fish (SG) = fish (PL)
```

```rust
MorphRule(
    conditions: [
        LemmaIs("sheep"),  // or specific lexemes
        NumberIs(Plural),
    ],
    operations: [],  // No change!
)
```

**2. Past tense: zero for some verbs**

```
cut (PRES) = cut (PAST)
put (PRES) = put (PAST)
hit (PRES) = hit (PAST)
```

**3. 3rd person singular: zero for modals**

```
can (1sg) = can (3sg)
must (1sg) = must (3sg)
should (1sg) = should (3sg)
```

### Representing Zero Morphology

```rust
pub struct ZeroMorphRule {
    /// When zero marking applies
    pub conditions: Vec<Condition>,
    /// Explicit marker that this is zero morphology
    pub zero: bool,
}
```

Zero morphology is just a rule with no operations. It's important to document these cases explicitly so the system knows when NOT to apply other rules.

---

## Morphological Ambiguity

A single surface form can correspond to multiple analyses. The parser must disambiguate based on context.

### Common Ambiguities (Polish)

**1. Case ambiguity**

```
"jabłko" → NOM.SG or ACC.SG (neuter)
"książki" → GEN.SG or NOM.PL or ACC.PL (feminine)
"domu" → GEN.SG or LOC.SG (masculine)
```

**Resolution:** Use verb subcategorization and word order.

```
"Tomek dał jabłko Izie"
  → "dać" requires [agent:NOM, theme:ACC, recipient:DAT]
  → "jabłko" must be ACC (theme), not NOM (agent)
```

**2. Person/number ambiguity**

```
"czyta" → 3SG.PRES or 2SG.IMPERATIVE
"czytaj" → 2SG.IMPERATIVE (unambiguous)
```

**Resolution:** Use sentence type (statement vs command).

**3. Gender ambiguity**

```
"dał" → 3SG.PAST.M (he gave)
"dała" → 3SG.PAST.F (she gave)
"dało" → 3SG.PAST.N (it gave)
```

These are unambiguous, but pronouns can be ambiguous:

```
"jego" → GEN.SG.M or ACC.SG.M (his/him)
"jej" → GEN.SG.F or DAT.SG.F (her/to her)
```

**Resolution:** Use syntactic position and verb requirements.

### Ambiguity Resolution Pipeline

```rust
pub fn resolve_morphological_ambiguity(
    form: &str,
    candidates: Vec<MorphAnalysis>,
    context: &ParseContext,
) -> MorphAnalysis {
    // Strategy 1: Verb subcategorization
    if let Some(verb_subcat) = context.verb_subcategorization {
        let filtered = candidates.iter()
            .filter(|c| verb_subcat.accepts(c.case))
            .collect::<Vec<_>>();
        
        if filtered.len() == 1 {
            return filtered[0].clone();
        }
    }
    
    // Strategy 2: Agreement with adjacent words
    if let Some(adj) = context.adjacent_adjective {
        let filtered = candidates.iter()
            .filter(|c| c.agrees_with(&adj))
            .collect::<Vec<_>>();
        
        if filtered.len() == 1 {
            return filtered[0].clone();
        }
    }
    
    // Strategy 3: Word order heuristics
    if context.position == SentencePosition::PreVerbal {
        // Pre-verbal position tends to be subject (NOM)
        let nom_candidates = candidates.iter()
            .filter(|c| c.case == Case::Nominative)
            .collect::<Vec<_>>();
        
        if nom_candidates.len() == 1 {
            return nom_candidates[0].clone();
        }
    }
    
    // Strategy 4: Frequency (most common form)
    candidates.iter()
        .max_by_key(|c| c.frequency)
        .unwrap()
        .clone()
}
```

---

## Summary

Advanced morphology features:

1. **Allomorphy** — variant forms determined by phonological, morphological, or lexical context
2. **Suppletion** — completely different roots for different forms (go/went/gone, good/better/best)
3. **Zero morphology** — grammatical categories expressed by no overt marking (neuter ACC = NOM)
4. **Morphological ambiguity** — single form with multiple analyses, resolved by context

These features are represented in the paradigm system through:
- `Allomorph` types for variant forms
- `SuppletiveParadigm` for completely different stems
- Empty `operations` for zero morphology
- Disambiguation pipeline using verb subcat, agreement, word order, and frequency

# Ontology — Concept Hierarchy and Semantic Relations

The ontology defines hierarchical relationships between concepts in the Interlingua. It enables semantic reasoning, type checking, and inference during parsing and generation.

## Concept Hierarchy

Concepts are organized in a tree structure using `IS_A` (hyponymy) and `PART_OF` (meronymy) relations.

```
PHYSICAL_OBJECT
├─ ANIMATE_ENTITY
│  ├─ PERSON
│  │  ├─ MALE_PERSON
```

## Ontology Data Structure

```rust
pub struct Ontology {
    /// Map from concept ID to concept definition
    pub concepts: HashMap<ConceptId, ConceptDefinition>,
    
    /// IS_A relations (child → parent)
    pub is_a: HashMap<ConceptId, Vec<ConceptId>>,
    
    /// PART_OF relations (part → whole)
    pub part_of: HashMap<ConceptId, Vec<ConceptId>>,
    
    /// Semantic type constraints
    pub type_constraints: Vec<TypeConstraint>,
}

pub struct ConceptDefinition {
    pub id: ConceptId,
    pub name: String,
    pub category: ConceptCategory,
    pub inherent_features: FeatureBundle,
    
    /// What frame this concept can participate in
    pub allowed_frames: Vec<FrameType>,
    
    /// What roles this concept can fill
    pub allowed_roles: Vec<SemanticRole>,
    
    /// Ontological properties
    pub properties: ConceptProperties,
}

pub struct ConceptProperties {
    /// Can this concept be counted? (apple: yes, water: no)
    pub countability: Countability,
    
    /// Is this concept concrete or abstract?
    pub concreteness: Concreteness,
    
    /// Typical size (for physical objects)
    pub typical_size: Option<Size>,
    
    /// Typical color (if characteristic)
    pub typical_color: Option<Color>,
}
```

## Type Constraints

Type constraints define what kinds of entities can fill specific roles in frames. This enables semantic validation during parsing.

```rust
pub struct TypeConstraint {
    /// Frame type this constraint applies to
    pub frame: FrameType,
    
    /// Role within the frame
    pub role: SemanticRole,
    
    /// Required semantic type (concept must be descendant of this)
    pub required_type: ConceptId,
    
    /// Optional: forbidden types (concept must NOT be descendant of this)
    pub forbidden_types: Vec<ConceptId>,
}
```

### Example Constraints

```rust
// GIVE frame constraints
TypeConstraint {
    frame: FrameType::Transfer,
    role: SemanticRole::Agent,
    required_type: ANIMATE_ENTITY,  // agent must be animate
    forbidden_types: vec![],
}

TypeConstraint {
    frame: FrameType::Transfer,
    role: SemanticRole::Theme,
    required_type: PHYSICAL_OBJECT,  // theme must be physical
    forbidden_types: vec![ABSTRACT_ENTITY],
}

// EAT frame constraints
TypeConstraint {
    frame: FrameType::Destruction,
    role: SemanticRole::Agent,
    required_type: ANIMATE_ENTITY,  // eater must be animate
    forbidden_types: vec![],
}

TypeConstraint {
    frame: FrameType::Destruction,
    role: SemanticRole::Theme,
    required_type: FOOD,  // eaten thing must be food
    forbidden_types: vec![],
}

// SEE frame constraints
TypeConstraint {
    frame: FrameType::Perception,
    role: SemanticRole::Agent,
    required_type: PERSON,  // seer must be person (has eyes)
    forbidden_types: vec![INANIMATE_OBJECT],
}

TypeConstraint {
    frame: FrameType::Perception,
    role: SemanticRole::Theme,
    required_type: PHYSICAL_OBJECT,  // seen thing must be physical
    forbidden_types: vec![ABSTRACT_ENTITY],
}
```

## Type Checking During Parsing

When parsing a sentence, the engine validates that entities match the type constraints for their roles:

```rust
pub fn validate_semantic_types(
    frame: &Frame,
    ontology: &Ontology,
) -> Result<(), SemanticError> {
    let frame_type = frame.frame_type();
    
    for (role, entity) in frame.roles.iter() {
        // Find applicable constraints
        let constraints = ontology.constraints_for(frame_type, *role);
        
        for constraint in constraints {
            let entity_concept = entity.concept;
            
            // Check required type
            if !ontology.is_descendant_of(entity_concept, constraint.required_type) {
                return Err(SemanticError::TypeMismatch {
                    role: *role,
                    expected: constraint.required_type,
                    found: entity_concept,
                });
            }
            
            // Check forbidden types
            for forbidden in &constraint.forbidden_types {
                if ontology.is_descendant_of(entity_concept, *forbidden) {
                    return Err(SemanticError::ForbiddenType {
                        role: *role,
                        forbidden: *forbidden,
                        found: entity_concept,
                    });
                }
            }
        }
    }
    
    Ok(())
}
```

### Example: Semantic Validation

```
Input: "Tomek zjadł jabłko."
       (Tomek ate an apple.)

Parsing:
  - Frame: Destruction (EAT)
  - Agent: Tomek (PERSON)
  - Theme: jabłko (APPLE)

Validation:
  - Constraint: EAT agent must be ANIMATE_ENTITY
    ✓ PERSON IS_A ANIMATE_ENTITY
    
  - Constraint: EAT theme must be FOOD
    ✓ APPLE IS_A FRUIT IS_A FOOD
    
  Result: VALID

---

Input: "Kamień zjadł jabłko."
       (The stone ate an apple.)

Parsing:
  - Frame: Destruction (EAT)
  - Agent: kamień (STONE → PHYSICAL_OBJECT)
  - Theme: jabłko (APPLE)

Validation:
  - Constraint: EAT agent must be ANIMATE_ENTITY
    ✗ PHYSICAL_OBJECT is NOT descendant of ANIMATE_ENTITY
      (STONE IS_A PHYSICAL_OBJECT IS_A INANIMATE_OBJECT)
    
  Result: SemanticError::TypeMismatch
          (stones cannot eat — semantic anomaly)
```

## Inheritance of Features

Concepts inherit features from their ancestors in the ontology:

```rust
impl Ontology {
    pub fn get_inherited_features(&self, concept: ConceptId) -> FeatureBundle {
        let mut features = FeatureBundle::default();
        
        // Collect features from all ancestors (bottom-up)
        let ancestors = self.get_ancestors(concept);
        
        for ancestor in ancestors.iter().rev() {
            let ancestor_def = self.concepts.get(ancestor).unwrap();
            features.merge_with(&ancestor_def.inherent_features);
        }
        
        // Override with concept's own features
        let concept_def = self.concepts.get(&concept).unwrap();
        features.merge_with(&concept_def.inherent_features);
        
        features
    }
}
```

### Example: Feature Inheritance

```
APPLE:
  - Own features: {gender: neuter, countability: count}

FRUIT (parent):
  - Inherent features: {concreteness: concrete}

FOOD (grandparent):
  - Inherent features: {edibility: edible}

PHYSICAL_OBJECT (great-grandparent):
  - Inherent features: {tangibility: tangible}

Inherited features for APPLE:
  {
    tangibility: tangible,      // from PHYSICAL_OBJECT
    edibility: edible,          // from FOOD
    concreteness: concrete,     // from FRUIT
    gender: neuter,             // own
    countability: count,        // own
  }
```

## IS_A Reasoning

The `IS_A` relation enables semantic inference:

```rust
impl Ontology {
    /// Check if concept_a IS_A concept_b
    /// (concept_a is a hyponym of concept_b)
    pub fn is_a(&self, concept_a: ConceptId, concept_b: ConceptId) -> bool {
        if concept_a == concept_b {
            return true;
        }
        
        // Check direct parents
        if let Some(parents) = self.is_a.get(&concept_a) {
            for parent in parents {
                if *parent == concept_b {
                    return true;
                }
                
                // Recursively check ancestors
                if self.is_a(*parent, concept_b) {
                    return true;
                }
            }
        }
        
        false
    }
    
    /// Check if concept_a is a descendant of concept_b
    /// (concept_b is an ancestor of concept_a)
    pub fn is_descendant_of(&self, concept_a: ConceptId, concept_b: ConceptId) -> bool {
        self.is_a(concept_a, concept_b)
    }
    
    /// Get all ancestors of a concept
    pub fn get_ancestors(&self, concept: ConceptId) -> Vec<ConceptId> {
        let mut ancestors = Vec::new();
        let mut current = concept;
        
        while let Some(parents) = self.is_a.get(&current) {
            if let Some(parent) = parents.first() {
                ancestors.push(*parent);
                current = *parent;
            } else {
                break;
            }
        }
        
        ancestors
    }
}
```

## PART_OF Reasoning

The `PART_OF` relation (meronymy) describes composition:

```rust
pub struct PartOfRelation {
    pub part: ConceptId,
    pub whole: ConceptId,
    pub relation_type: PartOfType,
}

pub enum PartOfType {
    /// Physical component (wheel PART_OF car)
    Component,
    
    /// Member of group (tree PART_OF forest)
    Member,
    
    /// Portion of substance (slice PART_OF cake)
    Portion,
}
```

### Example PART_OF Relations

```
Component:
  - WHEEL PART_OF CAR
  - PAGE PART_OF BOOK
  - HANDLE PART_OF DOOR

Member:
  - TREE PART_OF FOREST
  - PLAYER PART_OF TEAM
  - WORD PART_OF SENTENCE

Portion:
  - SLICE PART_OF CAKE
  - DROP PART_OF WATER
  - PIECE PART_OF BREAD
```

## Semantic Roles and Ontology

The ontology constrains which concepts can fill which semantic roles:

```rust
impl ConceptDefinition {
    /// Check if this concept can fill a given role
    pub fn can_fill_role(&self, role: SemanticRole) -> bool {
        self.allowed_roles.contains(&role)
    }
    
    /// Check if this concept can participate in a given frame
    pub fn can_participate_in(&self, frame: FrameType) -> bool {
        self.allowed_frames.contains(&frame)
    }
}
```

### Role Constraints by Concept Category

```
ANIMATE_ENTITY:
  - Can fill: Agent, Experiencer, Recipient, Beneficiary
  - Cannot fill: Theme (usually), Location, Instrument

PHYSICAL_OBJECT:
  - Can fill: Theme, Patient, Location, Instrument
  - Cannot fill: Agent (unless personified)

PERSON:
  - Can fill: Agent, Experiencer, Recipient, Beneficiary, Theme
  - Most flexible role assignment

ABSTRACT_ENTITY:
  - Can fill: Theme (in cognitive frames), Stimulus
  - Cannot fill: Agent, Location, Instrument
```

```ron
        TypeConstraint(frame: Emotion, role: Theme, required_type: "PHYSICAL_OBJECT", forbidden_types: []),
        
        // Communication frame (SAY, TELL, ASK)
        TypeConstraint(frame: Communication, role: Agent, required_type: "PERSON", forbidden_types: []),
        TypeConstraint(frame: Communication, role: Theme, required_type: "ABSTRACT_ENTITY", forbidden_types: []),
    ],
)
```

## Ontology Usage Examples

### 1. Semantic Validation

```rust
// Parse: "Kamień zjadł jabłko." (The stone ate an apple.)

let frame = Frame::Destruction {
    agent: Entity { concept: STONE, .. },
    theme: Entity { concept: APPLE, .. },
};

// Validate
let result = ontology.validate_semantic_types(&frame);

// Result: Err(SemanticError::TypeMismatch {
//   role: Agent,
//   expected: ANIMATE_ENTITY,
//   found: STONE  // STONE is INANIMATE, not ANIMATE
// })
```

### 2. Concept Generalization

```rust
// "Tomek dał jabłko Izie."
// "Tomek dał gruszkę Izie."
// "Tomek dał chleb Izie."

// All three themes are FOOD
// Can generalize: "Tomek dał jedzenie Izie." (Tomek gave food to Iza.)

let apple = ConceptId::APPLE;
let pear = ConceptId::PEAR;
let bread = ConceptId::BREAD;

let common_ancestor = ontology.find_common_ancestor(&[apple, pear, bread]);
// Result: Some(FOOD)
```

### 3. Semantic Similarity

```rust
// Calculate semantic distance between concepts
pub fn semantic_distance(&self, a: ConceptId, b: ConceptId) -> usize {
    let ancestors_a = self.get_ancestors(a);
    let ancestors_b = self.get_ancestors(b);
    
    // Find lowest common ancestor
    let lca = ancestors_a.iter()
        .find(|anc| ancestors_b.contains(anc))
        .copied();
    
    if let Some(lca) = lca {
        let dist_a = ancestors_a.iter().position(|x| *x == lca).unwrap_or(0);
        let dist_b = ancestors_b.iter().position(|x| *x == lca).unwrap_or(0);
        dist_a + dist_b
    } else {
        usize::MAX  // no common ancestor
    }
}

// distance(APPLE, PEAR) = 2 (APPLE→FRUIT←PEAR)
// distance(APPLE, DOG) = 6 (APPLE→FRUIT→FOOD→INANIMATE←ANIMATE←DOG)
```

## Ontology Maintenance

The ontology grows as new concepts are added to the lexicon:

```rust
impl Ontology {
    /// Add a new concept to the ontology
    pub fn add_concept(
        &mut self,
        concept: ConceptDefinition,
        parent: Option<ConceptId>,
    ) {
        let id = concept.id.clone();
        self.concepts.insert(id.clone(), concept);
        
        if let Some(parent_id) = parent {
            self.is_a.entry(id).or_insert_with(Vec::new).push(parent_id);
        }
    }
    
    /// Validate ontology consistency
    pub fn validate(&self) -> Result<(), OntologyError> {
        // Check for cycles in IS_A
        for concept in self.concepts.keys() {
            if self.has_cycle(*concept) {
                return Err(OntologyError::CycleDetected(*concept));
            }
        }
        
        // Check that all IS_A parents exist
        for (child, parents) in &self.is_a {
            for parent in parents {
                if !self.concepts.contains_key(parent) {
                    return Err(OntologyError::MissingConcept(*parent));
                }
            }
        }
        
        Ok(())
    }
}
```

## Summary

The ontology provides:

1. **Hierarchical organization** of concepts using IS_A and PART_OF relations
2. **Semantic type checking** to validate that entities match role constraints
3. **Feature inheritance** so concepts automatically inherit properties from ancestors
4. **Semantic reasoning** for inference and generalization
5. **Anomaly detection** to catch semantic errors like "stones eat apples"

The ontology is stored in `data/ontology/ontology.ron` and loaded at startup for use during parsing and generation.
<<<END_FILE>>>

# Performance Guide — Optimization and Benchmarks

This document provides guidance on performance optimization, benchmarking, and expected performance characteristics of lexFlex v0.1.

## Performance Targets

### v0.1 Performance Goals

| Metric | Target | Notes |
|--------|--------|-------|
| Translation latency | < 100ms | Per simple sentence |
| Memory usage | < 50MB | Full system loaded |
| Throughput | > 10 sentences/sec | Sustained |
| Startup time | < 500ms | Load all components |
| Accuracy | > 80% | On test suite |

### Performance Characteristics

**Complexity Analysis:**

| Component | Time Complexity | Space Complexity | Notes |
|-----------|----------------|------------------|-------|
| Tokenization | O(n) | O(n) | n = input length |
| Morphology | O(n × p) | O(p) | p = number of paradigms |
| Parsing | O(n²) | O(n²) | Dependency parsing |
| Deduction | O(n²) | O(n) | n = number of NPs |
| Generation | O(n) | O(n) | n = number of roles |

**Overall:** O(n²) for simple sentences, where n = number of words.

## Optimization Strategies

### 1. Morphology Optimization

**Problem:** Morphology lookup is O(n × p) where p = number of paradigms.

**Solution:** Use indexed lookup with caching.

```rust
pub struct OptimizedMorphology {
    // Index by lemma for fast lookup
    lemma_index: HashMap<String, Vec<MorphParadigm>>,
    
    // Cache recent lookups
    cache: LruCache<(String, FeatureBundle), String>,
}

impl OptimizedMorphology {
    pub fn inflect(
        &mut self,
        lemma: &str,
        features: &FeatureBundle,
    ) -> Result<String, MorphError> {
        // Check cache first
        let cache_key = (lemma.to_string(), features.clone());
        if let Some(cached) = self.cache.get(&cache_key) {
            return Ok(cached.clone());
        }
        
        // Look up paradigms
        let paradigms = self.lemma_index.get(lemma)
            .ok_or(MorphError::UnknownLemma(lemma.to_string()))?;
        
        // Apply first matching paradigm
        for paradigm in paradigms {
            if let Ok(result) = paradigm.apply(features) {
                // Cache result
                self.cache.put(cache_key, result.clone());
                return Ok(result);
            }
        }
        
        Err(MorphError::NoMatchingParadigm)
    }
}
```

**Expected improvement:** 10-20x speedup for repeated forms.

### 2. Lexicon Optimization

**Problem:** Lexicon lookup requires scanning all entries.

**Solution:** Use indexed lookup with concept ID.

```rust
pub struct OptimizedLexicon {
    // Index by concept ID for fast lookup
    concept_index: HashMap<ConceptId, Vec<LexEntry>>,
    
    // Index by lemma for reverse lookup
    lemma_index: HashMap<String, LexEntry>,
    
    // Index by language
    language_index: HashMap<LanguageId, HashMap<String, LexEntry>>,
}

impl OptimizedLexicon {
    pub fn lookup_by_concept(
        &self,
        concept: &ConceptId,
        language: LanguageId,
    ) -> Option<&LexEntry> {
        self.concept_index.get(concept)?
            .iter()
            .find(|entry| entry.language == language)
    }
    
    pub fn lookup_by_lemma(
        &self,
        lemma: &str,
        language: LanguageId,
    ) -> Option<&LexEntry> {
        self.language_index.get(&language)?
            .get(lemma)
    }
}
```

**Expected improvement:** O(1) lookup instead of O(n).

### 3. Deduction Optimization

**Problem:** Deduction is O(n²) due to role assignment.

**Solution:** Use early termination and priority queues.

```rust
pub fn optimize_deduction(
    sentence: &mut Sentence,
    context: &DeductionContext,
) -> Result<(), DeductionError> {
    
    // Step 1: Quick verb lookup
    let verb = find_main_verb(sentence)?;
    let frame_template = context.lexicon.lookup_verb(&verb.lemma)?;
    
    // Step 2: Collect NPs with explicit cases first
    let mut explicit_nps: Vec<_> = sentence.tokens.iter()
        .filter(|t| t.pos == PartOfSpeech::Noun && t.features.case.is_some())
        .collect();
    
    // Step 3: Assign explicit cases immediately (O(n))
    let mut assignments = Vec::new();
    for np in &explicit_nps {
        if let Some(role) = find_role_for_case(np.features.case.unwrap(), &frame_template) {
            assignments.push((role, np));
        }
    }
    
    // Step 4: Only process ambiguous NPs (usually 0-2)
    let ambiguous_nps: Vec<_> = sentence.tokens.iter()
        .filter(|t| t.pos == PartOfSpeech::Noun && t.features.case.is_none())
        .collect();
    
    // Step 5: Resolve ambiguities (O(k²) where k = number of ambiguous NPs)
    if ambiguous_nps.len() <= 2 {
        // Use simple algorithm for small k
        resolve_simple(&mut assignments, &ambiguous_nps, &frame_template)?;
    } else {
        // Use constraint solver for large k
        solve_constraints(&mut assignments, &ambiguous_nps, &frame_template)?;
    }
    
    Ok(())
}
```

**Expected improvement:** 5-10x speedup for typical sentences (k ≤ 2).

### 4. Caching Strategy

**Problem:** Repeated processing of same structures.

**Solution:** Multi-level caching.

```rust
pub struct TranslationCache {
    // Level 1: Full sentence cache
    sentence_cache: LruCache<String, String>,
    
    // Level 2: InterlinguaNode cache
    interlingua_cache: LruCache<String, InterlinguaNode>,
    
    // Level 3: Morphology cache (in OptimizedMorphology)
    // Already implemented
}

impl TranslationCache {
    pub fn translate_cached(
        &mut self,
        input: &str,
        from: LanguageId,
        to: LanguageId,
        translator: &UniversalTranslator,
    ) -> Result<String, TranslateError> {
        
        // Check sentence cache
        let cache_key = format!("{}:{}:{}", from, to, input);
        if let Some(cached) = self.sentence_cache.get(&cache_key) {
            return Ok(cached.clone());
        }
        
        // Check interlingua cache
        let il_key = format!("{}:{}", from, input);
        let il = if let Some(cached_il) = self.interlingua_cache.get(&il_key) {
            cached_il.clone()
        } else {
            let new_il = translator.parse(input, from)?;
            self.interlingua_cache.put(il_key, new_il.clone());
            new_il
        };
        
        // Generate
        let output = translator.generate(&il, to)?;
        
        // Cache result
        self.sentence_cache.put(cache_key, output.clone());
        
        Ok(output)
    }
}
```

**Expected improvement:** 2-5x speedup for repeated sentences.

### 5. Memory Optimization

**Problem:** Full system uses ~50MB.

**Solution:** Lazy loading and memory-mapped files.

```rust
pub struct LazyLexicon {
    // Memory-mapped file
    mmap: Mmap,
    
    // Index for fast lookup (loaded on demand)
    index: Option<HashMap<String, usize>>,
}

impl LazyLexicon {
    pub fn new(path: &Path) -> Result<Self, io::Error> {
        let file = File::open(path)?;
        let mmap = unsafe { Mmap::map(&file)? };
        
        Ok(Self {
            mmap,
            index: None,
        })
    }
    
    pub fn lookup(&mut self, lemma: &str) -> Option<&LexEntry> {
        // Build index on first access
        if self.index.is_none() {
            self.build_index();
        }
        
        // Use index for fast lookup
        let offset = self.index.as_ref()?.get(lemma)?;
        
        // Deserialize entry from mmap
        let entry: LexEntry = bincode::deserialize(&self.mmap[*offset..])?;
        Some(Box::leak(Box::new(entry)))
    }
}
```

**Expected improvement:** 50-70% memory reduction.

## Benchmarking

### Benchmark Suite

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod benchmarks {
    use super::*;
    use criterion::{black_box, criterion_group, criterion_main, Criterion};
    
    fn bench_tokenization(c: &mut Criterion) {
        let parser = PolishParser::new();
        let input = "Tomek dał jabłko Izie wczoraj";
        
        c.bench_function("tokenize_simple", |b| {
            b.iter(|| parser.tokenize(black_box(input)))
        });
    }
    
    fn bench_morphology(c: &mut Criterion) {
        let morphology = OptimizedMorphology::load("data/morphology/pl/").unwrap();
        let lemma = "jabłko";
        let features = FeatureBundle {
            case: Some(Case::Accusative),
            number: Some(Number::Singular),
            ..Default::default()
        };
        
        c.bench_function("inflect_noun", |b| {
            b.iter(|| morphology.inflect(black_box(lemma), black_box(&features)))
        });
    }
    
    fn bench_parsing(c: &mut Criterion) {
        let parser = PolishParser::new();
        let input = "Tomek dał jabłko Izie";
        
        c.bench_function("parse_simple", |b| {
            b.iter(|| parser.parse(black_box(input)))
        });
    }
    
    fn bench_deduction(c: &mut Criterion) {
        let parser = PolishParser::new();
        let context = DeductionContext::new();
        let utterance = parser.parse("Tomek dał jabłko Izie").unwrap();
        
        c.bench_function("deduce_simple", |b| {
            b.iter(|| deduction::deduce(black_box(utterance.clone()), &context))
        });
    }
    
    fn bench_generation(c: &mut Criterion) {
        let generator = PolishGenerator::new();
        let il = create_test_interlingua();
        
        c.bench_function("generate_simple", |b| {
            b.iter(|| generator.generate(black_box(&il)))
        });
    }
    
    fn bench_translation(c: &mut Criterion) {
        let translator = UniversalTranslator::new();
        let input = "Tomek dał jabłko Izie";
        
        c.bench_function("translate_pl_to_en", |b| {
            b.iter(|| translator.translate(black_box(input), LanguageId::PL, LanguageId::EN))
        });
    }
    
    criterion_group!(
        benches,
        bench_tokenization,
        bench_morphology,
        bench_parsing,
        bench_deduction,
        bench_generation,
        bench_translation,
    );
    criterion_main!(benches);
}
```

### Expected Benchmark Results

| Benchmark | Target | Actual (estimated) |
|-----------|--------|-------------------|
| Tokenization | < 1ms | ~0.5ms |
| Morphology inflection | < 1ms | ~0.8ms |
| Parsing (simple) | < 10ms | ~8ms |
| Deduction | < 20ms | ~15ms |
| Generation | < 10ms | ~7ms |
| Full translation | < 100ms | ~85ms |


### Common Bottlenecks

**1. Morphology lookup**
- **Symptom:** High CPU usage in `inflect_*` functions
- **Cause:** Scanning all paradigms for each word
- **Solution:** Use indexed lookup with caching (see Optimization #1)

**2. Deduction**
- **Symptom:** O(n²) scaling with sentence length
- **Cause:** Trying all role assignments
- **Solution:** Early termination for explicit cases (see Optimization #3)

**3. Lexicon lookup**
- **Symptom:** Slow concept-to-word mapping
- **Cause:** Linear scan of lexicon
- **Solution:** Use indexed lookup (see Optimization #2)

**4. Memory allocation**
- **Symptom:** High memory usage, frequent GC
- **Cause:** Creating many temporary objects
- **Solution:** Use object pools and arenas

## Performance Testing

### Load Testing

```rust
#[test]
fn test_sustained_throughput() {
    let translator = UniversalTranslator::new();
    let sentences = load_test_sentences(1000);
    
    let start = Instant::now();
    let mut count = 0;
    
    for sentence in &sentences {
        let result = translator.translate(sentence, LanguageId::PL, LanguageId::EN);
        assert!(result.is_ok());
        count += 1;
    }
    
    let elapsed = start.elapsed();
    let throughput = count as f64 / elapsed.as_secs_f64();
    
    println!("Throughput: {:.2} sentences/sec", throughput);
    assert!(throughput > 10.0, "Throughput too low: {:.2}", throughput);
}
```

### Stress Testing

```rust
#[test]
fn test_long_sentences() {
    let translator = UniversalTranslator::new();
    
    // Test with 50-word sentence
    let long_sentence = generate_long_sentence(50);
    let result = translator.translate(&long_sentence, LanguageId::PL, LanguageId::EN);
    
    assert!(result.is_ok());
}

#[test]
fn test_many_nps() {
    let translator = UniversalTranslator::new();
    
    // Test with 10 noun phrases
    let sentence = "Tomek dał jabłko Izie, książkę Markowi, kwiaty Annie, ...";
    let result = translator.translate(sentence, LanguageId::PL, LanguageId::EN);
    
    assert!(result.is_ok());
}
```

```rust
pub struct PerformanceMetrics {
    // Latency metrics
    pub translation_latency_ms: Histogram,
    pub parsing_latency_ms: Histogram,
    pub deduction_latency_ms: Histogram,
    pub generation_latency_ms: Histogram,

    pub error_types: Counter,
```

```rust
fn log_performance(metrics: &PerformanceMetrics) {
    log::info!(
        "Performance: latency={:.2}ms, throughput={:.2}s/s, memory={}MB, cache_hit={:.2}%",
        metrics.translation_latency_ms.mean(),
        metrics.sentences_per_second.value(),
        metrics.memory_usage_mb.value(),
        metrics.cache_hit_rate.value() * 100.0,
    );
}
```

- [ ] All benchmarks pass targets
- [ ] No memory leaks (run for 1 hour)
- [ ] Throughput > 10 sentences/sec sustained
- [ ] Memory usage < 50MB
- [ ] Startup time < 500ms
- [ ] No CPU hotspots in profiler

# Pronouns — Forms, Selection, and Placement

Pronouns are a complex area, especially in Polish. This document covers the pronoun system, enclitic forms, reflexives, and the algorithms for selecting the correct form and position.

## Pronoun Categories

```rust
pub enum PronounCategory {
    /// Personal pronouns: I, you, he, she, it, we, they
    Personal,
    
    /// Reflexive pronouns: myself, yourself, himself, siebie, sobie, się
    Reflexive,
    
    /// Possessive pronouns: my, your, his, her, its, our, their
    Possessive,
    
    /// Demonstrative pronouns: this, that, these, those
    Demonstrative,
    
    /// Interrogative pronouns: who, what, which
    Interrogative,
    
    /// Relative pronouns: who, which, that (in relative clauses)
    Relative,
}
```

## Polish Pronoun System

### Personal Pronouns

Polish personal pronouns have two forms: **full (stressed)** and **enclitic (unstressed)**.

```rust
pub struct PolishPronoun {
    pub person: Person,
    pub number: Number,
    pub gender: Option<Gender>,  // only for 3rd person
    pub case: Case,
    pub form_type: PronounFormType,
}

pub enum PronounFormType {
    /// Full form (stressed, can appear in any position)
    /// "mnie", "tobie", "jego", "jej", "nas", "was", "ich"
    Full,
    
    /// Enclitic form (unstressed, must follow verb)
    /// "mi", "ci", "go", "ją", "nam", "wam", "im"
    Enclitic,
    
    /// Special enclitic for accusative/genitive
    /// "niego", "niej", "nich" (after prepositions)
    PrepositionalEnclitic,
}
```

### Complete Pronoun Table

#### First Person Singular (ja)

| Case | Full Form | Enclitic | Example |
|------|-----------|----------|---------|
| Nominative | ja | — | **Ja** to zrobiłem |
| Genitive | mnie | — | nie ma **mnie** |
| Dative | mnie | **mi** | dał **mi** / **mnie** prezent |
| Accusative | mnie | — | widzi **mnie** |
| Instrumental | mną | — | idzie ze **mną** |
| Locative | mnie | — | myśli o **mnie** |

#### Second Person Singular (ty)

| Case | Full Form | Enclitic | Example |
|------|-----------|----------|---------|
| Nominative | ty | — | **Ty** to zrobiłeś |
| Genitive | ciebie | **cię** | nie ma **cię** |
| Dative | tobie | **ci** | dał **ci** / **tobie** prezent |
| Accusative | ciebie | **cię** | widzi **cię** |
| Instrumental | tobą | — | idzie z **tobą** |
| Locative | tobie | — | myśli o **tobie** |

| Case | Full Form | Enclitic | Prepositional | Example |
|------|-----------|----------|---------------|---------|
| Nominative | on | — | — | **On** przyszedł |
| Genitive | jego | **go** | niego | nie ma **go** / bez **niego** |
| Dative | jemu | **mu** | niemu | dał **mu** / **jemu** |
| Accusative | jego | **go** | niego | widzi **go** / dla **niego** |
| Instrumental | nim | — | — | idzie z **nim** |
| Locative | nim | — | — | myśli o **nim** |
